Text Standardization\nNormalize category names to enable matching across platforms.\n\n```python\nimport re\nfrom unicodedata import normalize\n\ndef standardize_text(text):\n    \"\"\"\n    Normalize category names:\n    - Remove extra whitespace\n    - Lowercase\n    - Remove special characters\n    - Handle unicode\n    \"\"\"\n    if not text or pd.isna(text):\n        return \"\"\n\n    # Unicode normalization\n    text = normalize('NFKD', str(text))\n\n    # Lowercase and strip\n    text = text.lower().strip()\n\n    # Remove special characters, keep alphanumeric and spaces\n    text = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9\\s]', '', text)\n\n    # Normalize whitespace\n    text = re.sub(r'\\s+', ' ', text)\n\n    return text\n\n# Apply to dataframe\ndf['standardized'] = df['category_name'].apply(standardize_text)\n```\n\n### 2. String Similarity Metrics\nMeasure similarity between category names.\n\n```python\nfrom difflib import SequenceMatcher\n\ndef similarity_ratio(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate similarity between two strings (0-1)\"\"\"\n    return SequenceMatcher(None, str1, str2).ratio()\n\n# Example\nprint(similarity_ratio(\"Laptop Computers\", \"Laptops\"))  # High similarity\n\n# Apply to pairs\nfrom itertools import combinations\npairs = combinations(categories, 2)\nsimilarities = [(c1, c2, similarity_ratio(c1, c2)) for c1, c2 in pairs]\n```\n\n### 3. Word Overlap Analysis\nIdentify semantic relationships between categories.\n\n```python\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap between strings\"\"\"\n    words1 = set(str1.lower().split())\n    words2 = set(str2.lower().split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n# Sibling validation: < 30% overlap\nassert word_overlap(\"laptops\", \"desktops\") < 0.3\n```\n\n### 4. Hierarchical Clustering\nGroup similar categories using clustering algorithms.\n\n```python\nfrom sklearn.cluster import AgglomerativeClustering\nfrom scipy.spatial.distance import pdist, squareform\nfrom sklearn.feature_extraction.text import TfidfVectorizer\n\ndef cluster_categories(categories, n_clusters, method='ward'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Cluster similar category names\n\n    Args:\n        categories: List of category names\n        n_clusters: Target number of clusters\n        method: 'ward', 'complete', 'average', 'single'\n    \"\"\"\n    # Convert to TF-IDF vectors\n    vectorizer = TfidfVectorizer(analyzer='char', ngram_range=(2, 2))\n    tfidf_matrix = vectorizer.fit_transform(categories)\n\n    # Calculate distances\n    distances = pdist(tfidf_matrix.toarray(), metric='cosine')\n    distance_matrix = squareform(distances)\n\n    # Perform clustering\n    clustering = AgglomerativeClustering(\n        n_clusters=n_clusters,\n        linkage=method,\n        metric='precomputed'\n    )\n\n    labels = clustering.fit_predict(distance_matrix)\n    return labels\n\n# Usage\ncategories = [\"Laptop\", \"Desktop\", \"Phone\", \"Tablet\"]\nclusters = cluster_categories(categories, n_clusters=2)\n```\n\n### 5. Mapping Categories\nCreate unified names for clustered categories.\n\n```python\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create unified category name from a group of similar categories.\n    Use most representative term.\n\n    Args:\n        category_group: List of category names in cluster\n        max_words: Maximum words in unified name\n    \"\"\"\n    # Find most common words\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        all_words.extend(cat.lower().split())\n\n    from collections import Counter\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Build name from most common words\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n# Example\ngroup = [\"laptop computers\", \"computer laptops\", \"portable computers\"]\nname = create_unified_name(group)  # \"laptop | computer\"\n```\n\n## Common Patterns\n\n### Source Distribution Check\n```python\ndef check_source_distribution(mapping_dict):\n    \"\"\"Verify even distribution of sources in clusters\"\"\"\n    from collections import Counter\n\n    for cluster_id, items in mapping_dict.items():\n        sources = Counter([item['source'] for item in items])\n        coverage = {s: count/len(items) for s, count in sources.items()}\n        print(f\"Cluster {cluster_id}: {coverage}\")\n```\n\n### Validation Rules\n```python\ndef validate_mapping(unified_cat, subcats):\n    \"\"\"Validate clustering meets business rules\"\"\"\n    # Rule: 70%+ categories should be representative\n    # Rule: < 30% word overlap between siblings\n    # Rule: No name overlap with parent\n    pass\n```\n\n## Performance Tips\n- Pre-compute all similarity matrices before clustering\n- Use TF-IDF with character n-grams for category name similarity\n- Cache standardized text to avoid repeated normalization\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":866,"cache_read_input_tokens":19108,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":866,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":24},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e16ceb26-c055-44e1-b1c5-b66f17ae68ec","uuid":"b93d1196-ff97-4fb3-995f-4dc59ec9c179"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_vrtx_01ESRAi5ek6G7HaTvXs4Z62T","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01JsTVxidqWppq1cJub1U6CG","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/taxonomy-building-validation.md","content":"# Taxonomy Building and Validation Skill\n\n## Overview\nBuilding hierarchical taxonomies with validation rules for balanced, evenly-distributed category structures.\n\n## Core Concepts\n\n### 1. Pyramid Structure Validation\nEnsure the taxonomy forms a proper pyramid with decreasing fanout at each level.\n\n```python\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(taxonomy_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate that taxonomy forms a pyramid:\n    - Level 1: 10-20 categories\n    - Level 2-N: 3-20 subcategories per parent\n    - Each level has more categories than previous (roughly)\n    \"\"\"\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        # Count unique non-null entries at this level\n        count = taxonomy_df[col].notna().sum()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n    # Validate pyramid shape\n    print(\"Category counts by level:\")\n    for level, count in counts_by_level.items():\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} categories\")\n\n    # Check constraints\n    assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \"Level 1 should have 10-20 categories\"\n\n    # Each level should expand reasonably\n    for level in range(2, 6):\n        fanout = counts_by_level[level] / counts_by_level[level - 1]\n        assert 1 < fanout < 20, f\"Level {level} fanout {fanout} out of range\"\n\n    return True\n\n# Usage\nvalidate_pyramid_structure(taxonomy_df)\n```\n\n### 2. Source Distribution Balance\nEnsure categories from different sources are evenly spread.\n\n```python\ndef validate_source_distribution(taxonomy_df):\n    \"\"\"\n    Verify that all sources (amazon, facebook, google) are\n    relatively evenly distributed across the taxonomy.\n    \"\"\"\n    from collections import Counter\n\n    source_counts = Counter(taxonomy_df['source'])\n    total = len(taxonomy_df)\n\n    print(\"Source distribution:\")\n    for source, count in source_counts.items():\n        percentage = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"  {source}: {count} ({percentage:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Validate relatively even distribution (within 2x of each other)\n    counts = list(source_counts.values())\n    ratio = max(counts) / min(counts)\n    assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source distribution imbalance: {ratio}x\"\n\n    return True\n```\n\n### 3. Naming Conventions\nApply naming rules and validate consistency.\n\n```python\ndef create_category_name(components, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Create unified category name from components.\n    Rules:\n    - Use \" | \" as separator\n    - Max 5 words total\n    - No repeated words\n    - Capitalize first letter of each component\n    \"\"\"\n    # Remove duplicates while preserving order\n    seen = set()\n    unique = []\n    for comp in components:\n        clean = comp.strip().lower()\n        if clean not in seen:\n            unique.append(clean)\n            seen.add(clean)\n\n    # Limit to max words\n    final = \" | \".join(unique[:max_words])\n\n    # Capitalize\n    final = \" | \".join(word.capitalize() for word in final.split(\" | \"))\n\n    return final\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(parent_name, child_names):\n    \"\"\"\n    Validate naming rules:\n    1. No overlap between parent and child names\n    2. No overlap between sibling names (< 30% word overlap)\n    3. Each name max 5 words\n    \"\"\"\n    parent_words = set(parent_name.lower().split())\n\n    for child in child_names:\n        child_words = set(child.lower().split())\n\n        # Rule: No parent-child word overlap\n        overlap = len(parent_words & child_words)\n        assert overlap == 0, f\"Child '{child}' overlaps with parent '{parent_name}'\"\n\n        # Rule: Max 5 words\n        word_count = len(child.split(\" | \"))\n        assert word_count <= 5, f\"Child '{child}' exceeds 5 words\"\n\n    # Rule: < 30% overlap between siblings\n    from itertools import combinations\n    for c1, c2 in combinations(child_names, 2):\n        words1 = set(c1.lower().split())\n        words2 = set(c2.lower().split())\n\n        if words1 and words2:\n            overlap_pct = len(words1 & words2) / max(len(words1), len(words2))\n            assert overlap_pct < 0.3, \\\n                f\"Siblings '{c1}' and '{c2}' have {overlap_pct:.1%} word overlap\"\n\n    return True\n```\n\n### 4. Representativeness Check\nVerify that unified categories are representative of their subcategories.\n\n```python\ndef calculate_representativeness(unified_cat, subcategories, source_data):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate how representative a unified category is of its subcategories.\n    A category is 70%+ representative if its terms appear in 70%+ of subcats.\n    \"\"\"\n    unified_words = set(unified_cat.lower().split(\" | \"))\n\n    if not unified_words:\n        return 0\n\n    matching_subcat_count = 0\n\n    for subcat in subcategories:\n        subcat_words = set(subcat.lower().split())\n        if unified_words & subcat_words:  # Any word matches\n            matching_subcat_count += 1\n\n    representativeness = matching_subcat_count / len(subcategories)\n    return representativeness\n\n# Usage\nrep = calculate_representativeness(\"Laptop | Computer\", [\"laptop\", \"laptop computer\"], {})\nassert rep >= 0.7, \"Not representative enough\"\n```\n\n### 5. Get unique categories at this level\n        level_cats = df[df[col].notna()][col].unique()\n        level_categories[level] = list(level_cats)\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {len(level_cats)} unique categories\")\n\n    return level_categories\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 4: SIMILARITY AND CLUSTERING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap\"\"\"\n    if not str1 or not str2:\n        return 0\n\n    words1 = set(str1.split())\n    words2 = set(str2.split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n\ndef group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Group similar categories using a greedy clustering approach.\n    Returns list of lists, where each inner list is a group of similar categories.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not categories:\n        return []\n\n    categories = list(categories)\n    groups = []\n    used = set()\n\n    # Sort by length (process shorter names first, they're often more representative)\n    sorted_cats = sorted(categories, key=lambda x: len(x.split()))\n\n    for cat in sorted_cats:\n        if cat in used:\n            continue\n\n        # Start a new group\n        group = [cat]\n        used.add(cat)\n\n        # Find similar categories\n        for other in sorted_cats:\n            if other in used:\n                continue\n\n            # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Need more granularity\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.3)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # Add level 1 to dataframe\n    df['unified_level_1'] = df['level_1'].map(level_1_mapping)\n\n    # Level 2-5: Build under each parent\n    for level_num in range(2, 6):\n        print(f\"  Processing Level {level_num}...\")\n        col = f'level_{level_num}'\n        parent_col = f'unified_level_{level_num - 1}'\n\n        unified_col = f'unified_level_{level_num}'\n\n        # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Group similar children\n                child_groups = group_similar_categories(children, similarity_threshold=0.45)\n\n                # Create unified names\n                for group in child_groups:\n                    unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n                    for cat in group:\n                        level_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n\n        df[unified_col] = df[col].map(level_mapping)\n\n        num_unified = df[unified_col].notna().sum()\n        print(f\"    Created unified categories for Level {level_num} ({num_unified} mappings)\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 5: VALIDATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate pyramid structure\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating pyramid structure...\")\n\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        count = df[col].notna().sum()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} categories\")\n\n    # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Check each level for proper naming\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        if col not in df.columns:\n            continue\n\n        cats = df[col].dropna().unique()\n\n        for cat in cats:\n            # Rule: Max 5 words (separated by |)\n            words = cat.split(' | ')\n            if len(words) > 5:\n                print(f\"  ✗ {cat} has {len(words)} words (max 5)\")\n                violations += 1\n\n    if violations == 0:\n        print(f\"  ✓ All naming rules valid\")\n\n    return violations == 0\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 6: OUTPUT GENERATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef generate_outputs(df):\n    \"\"\"Generate output CSV files\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nGenerating output files...\")\n\n    os.makedirs('/root/output', exist_ok=True)\n\n    # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Create unified name with capitalization\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n\ndef build_unified_hierarchy(df):\n    \"\"\"Build the unified 5-level hierarchy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nBuilding unified hierarchy...\")\n\n    # Strategy: cluster level by level, using parent relationships to guide clustering\n\n    # Level 1: Top-level categories (target 10-20)\n    print(\"  Processing Level 1...\")\n    level_1_cats = df[df['level_1'].notna()]['level_1'].unique()\n    level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.4)\n\n    # Adjust clustering to meet 10-20 target\n    while len(level_1_groups) < 10:\n        # Need to merge more groups\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.6)\n\n    if len(level_1_groups) > 20:\n        # Need more granularity\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.3)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # Add level 1 to dataframe\n    df['unified_level_1'] = df['level_1'].map(level_1_mapping)\n\n    # Level 2-5: Build under each parent\n    for level_num in range(2, 6):\n        print(f\"  Processing Level {level_num}...\")\n        col = f'level_{level_num}'\n        parent_col = f'unified_level_{level_num - 1}'\n\n        unified_col = f'unified_level_{level_num}'\n\n        # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Group similar children\n                child_groups = group_similar_categories(children, similarity_threshold=0.45)\n\n                # Create unified names\n                for group in child_groups:\n                    unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n                    for cat in group:\n                        level_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n\n        df[unified_col] = df[col].map(level_mapping)\n\n        num_unified = df[unified_col].notna().sum()\n        print(f\"    Created unified categories for Level {level_num} ({num_unified} mappings)\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 5: VALIDATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate pyramid structure\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating pyramid structure...\")\n\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        count = df[col].notna().sum()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} categories\")\n\n    # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Check each level for proper naming\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        if col not in df.columns:\n            continue\n\n        cats = df[col].dropna().unique()\n\n        for cat in cats:\n            # Rule: Max 5 words (separated by |)\n            words = cat.split(' | ')\n            if len(words) > 5:\n                print(f\"  ✗ {cat} has {len(words)} words (max 5)\")\n                violations += 1\n\n    if violations == 0:\n        print(f\"  ✓ All naming rules valid\")\n\n    return violations == 0\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 6: OUTPUT GENERATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef generate_outputs(df):\n    \"\"\"Generate output CSV files\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nGenerating output files...\")\n\n    os.makedirs('/root/output', exist_ok=True)\n\n    # Output 1: Full mapping\n    output_df = df[[\n        'source',\n        'original_category_path',\n        'depth',\n        'unified_level_1',\n        'unified_level_2',\n        'unified_level_3',\n        'unified_level_4',\n        'unified_level_5'\n    ]].copy()\n\n    output_df = output_df.rename(columns={\n        'original_category_path': 'category_path'\n    })\n\n    output_df.to_csv('/root/output/unified_taxonomy_full.csv', index=False)\n    print(f\"  ✓ Wrote unified_taxonomy_full.csv ({len(output_df)} rows)\")\n\n    # Output 2: Hierarchy (unique paths only)\n    hierarchy_cols = [\n        'unified_level_1',\n        'unified_level_2',\n        'unified_level_3',\n        'unified_level_4',\n        'unified_level_5'\n    ]\n\n    # Get unique combinations\n    hierarchy_df = output_df[hierarchy_cols].drop_duplicates()\n\n    # Sort by level\n    hierarchy_df = hierarchy_df.sort_values(\n        by=[c for c in hierarchy_cols if c in hierarchy_df.columns],\n        na_position='last'\n    )\n\n    hierarchy_df.to_csv('/root/output/unified_taxonomy_hierarchy.csv', index=False)\n    print(f\"  ✓ Wrote unified_taxonomy_hierarchy.csv ({len(hierarchy_df)} rows)\")\n\n    return output_df, hierarchy_df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MAIN EXECUTION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"UNIFIED PRODUCT CATEGORY TAXONOMY BUILDER\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    # Validation\n    validate_pyramid_structure(df)\n    validate_source_distribution(df)\n    validate_naming_rules(df)\n\n    # Generate outputs\n    output_full, output_hierarchy = generate_outputs(df)\n\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"TASK COMPLETE!\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(f\"\\nOutput files generated:\")\n    print(f\"  - /root/output/unified_taxonomy_full.csv\")\n    print(f\"  - /root/output/unified_taxonomy_hierarchy.csv\")\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Get unique categories at this level\n        level_cats = df[df[col].notna()][col].unique()\n        level_categories[level] = list(level_cats)\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {len(level_cats)} unique categories\")\n\n    return level_categories\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 4: SIMILARITY AND CLUSTERING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap\"\"\"\n    if not str1 or not str2:\n        return 0\n\n    words1 = set(str1.split())\n    words2 = set(str2.split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n\ndef group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Group similar categories using a greedy clustering approach.\n    Returns list of lists, where each inner list is a group of similar categories.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not categories:\n        return []\n\n    categories = list(categories)\n    groups = []\n    used = set()\n\n    # Sort by length (process shorter names first, they're often more representative)\n    sorted_cats = sorted(categories, key=lambda x: len(x.split()))\n\n    for cat in sorted_cats:\n        if cat in used:\n            continue\n\n        # Start a new group\n        group = [cat]\n        used.add(cat)\n\n        # Find similar categories\n        for other in sorted_cats:\n            if other in used:\n                continue\n\n            # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Create unified name with capitalization\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n\ndef build_unified_hierarchy(df):\n    \"\"\"Build the unified 5-level hierarchy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nBuilding unified hierarchy...\")\n\n    # Strategy: cluster level by level, using parent relationships to guide clustering\n\n    # Level 1: Top-level categories (target 10-20)\n    print(\"  Processing Level 1...\")\n    level_1_cats = df[df['level_1'].notna()]['level_1'].unique()\n    level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.4)\n\n    # Adjust clustering to meet 10-20 target\n    while len(level_1_groups) < 10:\n        # Need to merge more groups\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.6)\n\n    if len(level_1_groups) > 20:\n        # Need more granularity\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.3)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # Add level 1 to dataframe\n    df['unified_level_1'] = df['level_1'].map(level_1_mapping)\n\n    # Level 2-5: Build under each parent\n    for level_num in range(2, 6):\n        print(f\"  Processing Level {level_num}...\")\n        col = f'level_{level_num}'\n        parent_col = f'unified_level_{level_num - 1}'\n\n        unified_col = f'unified_level_{level_num}'\n\n        # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Group similar children\n                child_groups = group_similar_categories(children, similarity_threshold=0.45)\n\n                # Create unified names\n                for group in child_groups:\n                    unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n                    for cat in group:\n                        level_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n\n        df[unified_col] = df[col].map(level_mapping)\n\n        num_unified = df[unified_col].notna().sum()\n        print(f\"    Created unified categories for Level {level_num} ({num_unified} mappings)\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 5: VALIDATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate pyramid structure\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating pyramid structure...\")\n\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        count = df[col].notna().sum()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} categories\")\n\n    # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Unicode normalization\n    text = normalize('NFKD', str(text))\n\n    # Lowercase and strip\n    text = text.lower().strip()\n\n    # Remove special characters, keep alphanumeric and spaces\n    text = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9\\s]', '', text)\n\n    # Normalize whitespace\n    text = re.sub(r'\\s+', ' ', text)\n\n    return text\n\n\ndef parse_category_path(path_str, delimiter=' > '):\n    \"\"\"Parse category path string into individual levels\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(path_str):\n        return []\n    parts = str(path_str).split(delimiter)\n    return [standardize_text(p) for p in parts if p.strip()]\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 2: DATA LOADING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef load_data():\n    \"\"\"Load data from all three sources\"\"\"\n    print(\"Loading data from all sources...\")\n\n    # Amazon\n    amazon_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/amazon_product_categories.csv')\n    amazon_df['source'] = 'amazon'\n    amazon_df = amazon_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n\n    # Facebook\n    fb_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/fb_product_categories.csv')\n    fb_df['source'] = 'facebook'\n    fb_df = fb_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n    fb_df = fb_df[['original_category_path', 'source']]\n\n    # Combine all\n    df = pd.concat([amazon_df[['original_category_path', 'source']],\n                    fb_df[['original_category_path', 'source']],\n                    google_df[['original_category_path', 'source']]],\n                   ignore_index=True)\n\n    # Get unique categories at this level\n        level_cats = df[df[col].notna()][col].unique()\n        level_categories[level] = list(level_cats)\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {len(level_cats)} unique categories\")\n\n    return level_categories\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 4: SIMILARITY AND CLUSTERING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap\"\"\"\n    if not str1 or not str2:\n        return 0\n\n    words1 = set(str1.split())\n    words2 = set(str2.split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n\ndef group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Group similar categories using a greedy clustering approach.\n    Returns list of lists, where each inner list is a group of similar categories.\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n    if len(categories) == 0:\n        return []\n    groups = []\n    used = set()\n\n    # Sort by length (process shorter names first, they're often more representative)\n    sorted_cats = sorted(categories, key=lambda x: len(x.split()))\n\n    for cat in sorted_cats:\n        if cat in used:\n            continue\n\n        # Start a new group\n        group = [cat]\n        used.add(cat)\n\n        # Find similar categories\n        for other in sorted_cats:\n            if other in used:\n                continue\n\n            # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Create unified name with capitalization\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n\ndef build_unified_hierarchy(df):\n    \"\"\"Build the unified 5-level hierarchy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nBuilding unified hierarchy...\")\n\n    # Strategy: cluster level by level, using parent relationships to guide clustering\n\n    # Level 1: Top-level categories (target 10-20)\n    print(\"  Processing Level 1...\")\n    level_1_cats = df[df['level_1'].notna()]['level_1'].unique()\n    level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.4)\n\n    # Adjust clustering to meet 10-20 target\n    while len(level_1_groups) < 10:\n        # Need to merge more groups\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.6)\n\n    if len(level_1_groups) > 20:\n        # Need more granularity\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.3)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Rule: Max 5 words (separated by |)\n            words = cat.split(' | ')\n            if len(words) > 5:\n                print(f\"  ✗ {cat} has {len(words)} words (max 5)\")\n                violations += 1\n\n    if violations == 0:\n        print(f\"  ✓ All naming rules valid\")\n\n    return violations == 0\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 6: OUTPUT GENERATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef generate_outputs(df):\n    \"\"\"Generate output CSV files\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nGenerating output files...\")\n\n    os.makedirs('/root/output', exist_ok=True)\n\n    # Unicode normalization\n    text = normalize('NFKD', str(text))\n\n    # Lowercase and strip\n    text = text.lower().strip()\n\n    # Remove special characters, keep alphanumeric and spaces\n    text = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9\\s]', '', text)\n\n    # Normalize whitespace\n    text = re.sub(r'\\s+', ' ', text)\n\n    return text\n\n\ndef parse_category_path(path_str, delimiter=' > '):\n    \"\"\"Parse category path string into individual levels\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(path_str):\n        return []\n    parts = str(path_str).split(delimiter)\n    return [standardize_text(p) for p in parts if p.strip()]\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 2: DATA LOADING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef load_data():\n    \"\"\"Load data from all three sources\"\"\"\n    print(\"Loading data from all sources...\")\n\n    # Amazon\n    amazon_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/amazon_product_categories.csv')\n    amazon_df['source'] = 'amazon'\n    amazon_df = amazon_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n\n    # Facebook\n    fb_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/fb_product_categories.csv')\n    fb_df['source'] = 'facebook'\n    fb_df = fb_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n    fb_df = fb_df[['original_category_path', 'source']]\n\n    # Google Shopping\n    google_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/google_shopping_product_categories.csv')\n    google_df['source'] = 'google'\n    google_df = google_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n\n    # Combine all\n    df = pd.concat([amazon_df[['original_category_path', 'source']],\n                    fb_df[['original_category_path', 'source']],\n                    google_df[['original_category_path', 'source']]],\n                   ignore_index=True)\n\n    # Remove duplicates\n    df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['original_category_path', 'source'])\n\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(amazon_df)} Amazon categories\")\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(fb_df)} Facebook categories\")\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(google_df)} Google categories\")\n    print(f\"Total unique: {len(df)}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\ndef prepare_hierarchies(df):\n    \"\"\"Parse category paths and extract levels\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nParsing category hierarchies...\")\n\n    # Parse paths\n    df['levels'] = df['original_category_path'].apply(parse_category_path)\n    df['depth'] = df['levels'].apply(len)\n\n    # Expand to individual columns\n    for i in range(1, 6):\n        df[f'level_{i}'] = df['levels'].apply(\n            lambda x: x[i-1] if i <= len(x) else None\n        )\n\n    print(f\"Category depths: {df['depth'].value_counts().sort_index().to_dict()}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 3: COLLECT UNIQUE CATEGORIES BY LEVEL\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef collect_level_categories(df):\n    \"\"\"Collect unique categories at each level, per source\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nCollecting unique categories by level...\")\n\n    level_categories = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'level_{level}'\n        # Get unique categories at this level\n        level_cats = df[df[col].notna()][col].unique()\n        level_categories[level] = list(level_cats)\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {len(level_cats)} unique categories\")\n\n    return level_categories\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 4: SIMILARITY AND CLUSTERING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap\"\"\"\n    if not str1 or not str2:\n        return 0\n\n    words1 = set(str1.split())\n    words2 = set(str2.split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n\ndef group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Group similar categories using a greedy clustering approach.\n    Returns list of lists, where each inner list is a group of similar categories.\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n    if len(categories) == 0:\n        return []\n    groups = []\n    used = set()\n\n    # Sort by length (process shorter names first, they're often more representative)\n    sorted_cats = sorted(categories, key=lambda x: len(x.split()))\n\n    for cat in sorted_cats:\n        if cat in used:\n            continue\n\n        # Start a new group\n        group = [cat]\n        used.add(cat)\n\n        # Find similar categories\n        for other in sorted_cats:\n            if other in used:\n                continue\n\n            # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Create unified name with capitalization\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n\ndef cluster_with_target(categories, target_clusters, min_threshold=0.2, max_threshold=0.9):\n    \"\"\"\n    Cluster categories to target number of clusters using binary search on threshold.\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n\n    # Binary search for the right threshold\n    low, high = min_threshold, max_threshold\n    best_threshold = 0.5\n    best_groups = []\n\n    for _ in range(10):  # Binary search iterations\n        mid = (low + high) / 2\n        groups = group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=mid)\n\n        if len(groups) == target_clusters:\n            return groups\n        elif len(groups) > target_clusters:\n            # Need higher threshold to merge more\n            low = mid\n        else:\n            # Need lower threshold to split more\n            high = mid\n\n        best_groups = groups\n        best_threshold = mid\n\n    return best_groups\n\n\ndef build_unified_hierarchy(df):\n    \"\"\"Build the unified 5-level hierarchy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nBuilding unified hierarchy...\")\n\n    # Level 1: Top-level categories (target 10-20)\n    print(\"  Processing Level 1...\")\n    level_1_cats = df[df['level_1'].notna()]['level_1'].unique()\n\n    # Try to get 15 clusters (middle of 10-20 range)\n    level_1_groups = cluster_with_target(level_1_cats, target_clusters=15)\n\n    # If still outside range, adjust\n    if len(level_1_groups) < 10:\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.7)\n    elif len(level_1_groups) > 20:\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.2)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # Add level 1 to dataframe\n    df['unified_level_1'] = df['level_1'].map(level_1_mapping)\n\n    # Level 2-5: Build under each parent\n    for level_num in range(2, 6):\n        print(f\"  Processing Level {level_num}...\")\n        col = f'level_{level_num}'\n        parent_col = f'unified_level_{level_num - 1}'\n\n        unified_col = f'unified_level_{level_num}'\n\n        # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Target 3-20 clusters per parent (aim for 8)\n                target = min(max(len(children) // 2, 3), 20)\n                child_groups = cluster_with_target(children, target_clusters=target)\n\n                # Create unified names\n                for group in child_groups:\n                    unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n                    for cat in group:\n                        level_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n\n        df[unified_col] = df[col].map(level_mapping)\n\n        num_unified = df[unified_col].notna().sum()\n        print(f\"    Created unified categories for Level {level_num} ({num_unified} mappings)\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 5: VALIDATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate pyramid structure\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating pyramid structure...\")\n\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        count = df[col].notna().sum()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} categories\")\n\n    # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Unicode normalization\n    text = normalize('NFKD', str(text))\n\n    # Lowercase and strip\n    text = text.lower().strip()\n\n    # Remove special characters, keep alphanumeric and spaces\n    text = re.sub(r'[^a-z0-9\\s]', '', text)\n\n    # Normalize whitespace\n    text = re.sub(r'\\s+', ' ', text)\n\n    return text\n\n\ndef parse_category_path(path_str, delimiter=' > '):\n    \"\"\"Parse category path string into individual levels\"\"\"\n    if pd.isna(path_str):\n        return []\n    parts = str(path_str).split(delimiter)\n    return [standardize_text(p) for p in parts if p.strip()]\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 2: DATA LOADING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef load_data():\n    \"\"\"Load data from all three sources\"\"\"\n    print(\"Loading data from all sources...\")\n\n    # Amazon\n    amazon_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/amazon_product_categories.csv')\n    amazon_df['source'] = 'amazon'\n    amazon_df = amazon_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n\n    # Facebook\n    fb_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/fb_product_categories.csv')\n    fb_df['source'] = 'facebook'\n    fb_df = fb_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n    fb_df = fb_df[['original_category_path', 'source']]\n\n    # Google Shopping\n    google_df = pd.read_csv('/root/data/google_shopping_product_categories.csv')\n    google_df['source'] = 'google'\n    google_df = google_df.rename(columns={\n        'category_path': 'original_category_path'\n    })\n\n    # Combine all\n    df = pd.concat([amazon_df[['original_category_path', 'source']],\n                    fb_df[['original_category_path', 'source']],\n                    google_df[['original_category_path', 'source']]],\n                   ignore_index=True)\n\n    # Parse paths\n    df['levels'] = df['original_category_path'].apply(parse_category_path)\n    df['depth'] = df['levels'].apply(len)\n\n    # Expand to individual columns\n    for i in range(1, 6):\n        df[f'level_{i}'] = df['levels'].apply(\n            lambda x: x[i-1] if i <= len(x) else None\n        )\n\n    print(f\"Category depths: {df['depth'].value_counts().sort_index().to_dict()}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 3: COLLECT UNIQUE CATEGORIES BY LEVEL\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef collect_level_categories(df):\n    \"\"\"Collect unique categories at each level, per source\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nCollecting unique categories by level...\")\n\n    level_categories = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'level_{level}'\n        # Get unique categories at this level\n        level_cats = df[df[col].notna()][col].unique()\n        level_categories[level] = list(level_cats)\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {len(level_cats)} unique categories\")\n\n    return level_categories\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 4: SIMILARITY AND CLUSTERING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap\"\"\"\n    if not str1 or not str2:\n        return 0\n\n    words1 = set(str1.split())\n    words2 = set(str2.split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n\ndef group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Group similar categories using a greedy clustering approach.\n    Returns list of lists, where each inner list is a group of similar categories.\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n    if len(categories) == 0:\n        return []\n    groups = []\n    used = set()\n\n    # Sort by length (process shorter names first, they're often more representative)\n    sorted_cats = sorted(categories, key=lambda x: len(x.split()))\n\n    for cat in sorted_cats:\n        if cat in used:\n            continue\n\n        # Start a new group\n        group = [cat]\n        used.add(cat)\n\n        # Find similar categories\n        for other in sorted_cats:\n            if other in used:\n                continue\n\n            # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Create unified name with capitalization\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n\ndef cluster_with_target(categories, target_clusters, min_threshold=0.2, max_threshold=0.9):\n    \"\"\"\n    Cluster categories to target number of clusters using binary search on threshold.\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n\n    # Binary search for the right threshold\n    low, high = min_threshold, max_threshold\n    best_threshold = 0.5\n    best_groups = []\n\n    for _ in range(10):  # Binary search iterations\n        mid = (low + high) / 2\n        groups = group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=mid)\n\n        if len(groups) == target_clusters:\n            return groups\n        elif len(groups) > target_clusters:\n            # Need higher threshold to merge more\n            low = mid\n        else:\n            # Need lower threshold to split more\n            high = mid\n\n        best_groups = groups\n        best_threshold = mid\n\n    return best_groups\n\n\ndef build_unified_hierarchy(df):\n    \"\"\"Build the unified 5-level hierarchy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nBuilding unified hierarchy...\")\n\n    # Level 1: Top-level categories (target 10-20)\n    print(\"  Processing Level 1...\")\n    level_1_cats = df[df['level_1'].notna()]['level_1'].unique()\n\n    # Try to get 15 clusters (middle of 10-20 range)\n    level_1_groups = cluster_with_target(level_1_cats, target_clusters=15)\n\n    # If still outside range, adjust\n    if len(level_1_groups) < 10:\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.7)\n    elif len(level_1_groups) > 20:\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.2)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # Add level 1 to dataframe\n    df['unified_level_1'] = df['level_1'].map(level_1_mapping)\n\n    # Level 2-5: Build under each parent\n    for level_num in range(2, 6):\n        print(f\"  Processing Level {level_num}...\")\n        col = f'level_{level_num}'\n        parent_col = f'unified_level_{level_num - 1}'\n\n        unified_col = f'unified_level_{level_num}'\n\n        # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Target 3-20 clusters per parent (aim for 8)\n                target = min(max(len(children) // 2, 3), 20)\n                child_groups = cluster_with_target(children, target_clusters=target)\n\n                # Create unified names\n                for group in child_groups:\n                    unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n                    for cat in group:\n                        level_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n\n        df[unified_col] = df[col].map(level_mapping)\n\n        num_unified = df[unified_col].notna().sum()\n        print(f\"    Created unified categories for Level {level_num} ({num_unified} mappings)\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 5: VALIDATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate pyramid structure\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating pyramid structure...\")\n\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        # Count UNIQUE unified categories at this level, not rows\n        count = df[col].nunique()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} unique categories\")\n\n    # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Rule: Max 5 words (separated by |)\n            words = cat.split(' | ')\n            if len(words) > 5:\n                print(f\"  ✗ {cat} has {len(words)} words (max 5)\")\n                violations += 1\n\n    if violations == 0:\n        print(f\"  ✓ All naming rules valid\")\n\n    return violations == 0\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 6: OUTPUT GENERATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef generate_outputs(df):\n    \"\"\"Generate output CSV files\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nGenerating output files...\")\n\n    os.makedirs('/root/output', exist_ok=True)\n\n    # Get unique combinations\n    hierarchy_df = output_df[hierarchy_cols].drop_duplicates()\n\n    # Sort by level\n    hierarchy_df = hierarchy_df.sort_values(\n        by=[c for c in hierarchy_cols if c in hierarchy_df.columns],\n        na_position='last'\n    )\n\n    hierarchy_df.to_csv('/root/output/unified_taxonomy_hierarchy.csv', index=False)\n    print(f\"  ✓ Wrote unified_taxonomy_hierarchy.csv ({len(hierarchy_df)} rows)\")\n\n    return output_df, hierarchy_df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# MAIN EXECUTION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef main():\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"UNIFIED PRODUCT CATEGORY TAXONOMY BUILDER\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    # Remove duplicates\n    df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['original_category_path', 'source'])\n\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(amazon_df)} Amazon categories\")\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(fb_df)} Facebook categories\")\n    print(f\"Loaded {len(google_df)} Google categories\")\n    print(f\"Total unique: {len(df)}\")\n\n    return df\n\n\ndef prepare_hierarchies(df):\n    \"\"\"Parse category paths and extract levels\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nParsing category hierarchies...\")\n\n    # Get unique categories at this level\n        level_cats = df[df[col].notna()][col].unique()\n        level_categories[level] = list(level_cats)\n        print(f\"  Level {level}: {len(level_cats)} unique categories\")\n\n    return level_categories\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 4: SIMILARITY AND CLUSTERING\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef word_overlap(str1, str2):\n    \"\"\"Calculate percentage of word overlap\"\"\"\n    if not str1 or not str2:\n        return 0\n\n    words1 = set(str1.split())\n    words2 = set(str2.split())\n\n    if not words1 or not words2:\n        return 0\n\n    overlap = len(words1 & words2)\n    max_words = max(len(words1), len(words2))\n\n    return overlap / max_words\n\n\ndef group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=0.5):\n    \"\"\"\n    Group similar categories using a greedy clustering approach.\n    Returns list of lists, where each inner list is a group of similar categories.\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n    if len(categories) == 0:\n        return []\n    groups = []\n    used = set()\n\n    # Sort by length (process shorter names first, they're often more representative)\n    sorted_cats = sorted(categories, key=lambda x: len(x.split()))\n\n    for cat in sorted_cats:\n        if cat in used:\n            continue\n\n        # Start a new group\n        group = [cat]\n        used.add(cat)\n\n        # Find similar categories\n        for other in sorted_cats:\n            if other in used:\n                continue\n\n            # Check similarity\n            overlap = word_overlap(cat, other)\n            if overlap >= similarity_threshold:\n                group.append(other)\n                used.add(other)\n\n        groups.append(group)\n\n    return groups\n\n\ndef create_unified_name(category_group, max_words=5):\n    \"\"\"Create unified category name from a group\"\"\"\n    # Get all words and their frequency\n    all_words = []\n    for cat in category_group:\n        words = cat.split()\n        all_words.extend(words)\n\n    word_counts = Counter(all_words)\n\n    # Get most common words (up to max_words)\n    top_words = [word for word, _ in word_counts.most_common(max_words)]\n\n    # Create unified name with capitalization\n    unified = \" | \".join(top_words)\n\n    return unified\n\n\ndef cluster_with_target(categories, target_clusters, min_threshold=0.0, max_threshold=1.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Cluster categories to target number of clusters using binary search on threshold.\n    Higher threshold = more aggressive merging (fewer clusters).\n    \"\"\"\n    categories = list(categories)\n    if len(categories) <= target_clusters:\n        return [[c] for c in categories]\n\n    # Binary search for the right threshold\n    low, high = min_threshold, max_threshold\n    best_groups = []\n    best_diff = float('inf')\n\n    for iteration in range(15):  # More iterations for finer granularity\n        mid = (low + high) / 2\n        groups = group_similar_categories(categories, similarity_threshold=mid)\n\n        diff = abs(len(groups) - target_clusters)\n\n        if diff < best_diff:\n            best_diff = diff\n            best_groups = groups\n\n        if len(groups) == target_clusters:\n            return groups\n        elif len(groups) > target_clusters:\n            # Too many clusters, need LOWER threshold to merge more\n            high = mid\n        else:\n            # Too few clusters, need HIGHER threshold to keep more separate\n            low = mid\n\n    return best_groups\n\n\ndef build_unified_hierarchy(df):\n    \"\"\"Build the unified 5-level hierarchy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nBuilding unified hierarchy...\")\n\n    # Level 1: Top-level categories (target 10-20)\n    print(\"  Processing Level 1...\")\n    level_1_cats = df[df['level_1'].notna()]['level_1'].unique()\n\n    # Try to get 15 clusters (middle of 10-20 range)\n    level_1_groups = cluster_with_target(level_1_cats, target_clusters=15)\n\n    # If still outside range, adjust\n    if len(level_1_groups) < 10:\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.7)\n    elif len(level_1_groups) > 20:\n        level_1_groups = group_similar_categories(level_1_cats, similarity_threshold=0.2)\n\n    print(f\"    Created {len(level_1_groups)} groups at Level 1\")\n\n    # Create mapping for Level 1\n    level_1_mapping = {}\n    level_1_unified_names = {}\n\n    for group in level_1_groups:\n        unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n        for cat in group:\n            level_1_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n            level_1_unified_names[unified_name] = group\n\n    # Add level 1 to dataframe\n    df['unified_level_1'] = df['level_1'].map(level_1_mapping)\n\n    # Level 2-5: Build under each parent\n    for level_num in range(2, 6):\n        print(f\"  Processing Level {level_num}...\")\n        col = f'level_{level_num}'\n        parent_col = f'unified_level_{level_num - 1}'\n\n        unified_col = f'unified_level_{level_num}'\n\n        # For each parent, group its children\n        if df[col].notna().sum() == 0:\n            continue\n\n        level_mapping = {}\n\n        for parent in df[parent_col].dropna().unique():\n            # Get all children under this parent\n            children = df[df[parent_col] == parent][col].dropna().unique()\n\n            if len(children) <= 5:\n                # Don't cluster, use original names\n                for child in children:\n                    level_mapping[child] = child\n            else:\n                # Target 3-20 clusters per parent (aim for 8)\n                target = min(max(len(children) // 2, 3), 20)\n                child_groups = cluster_with_target(children, target_clusters=target)\n\n                # Create unified names\n                for group in child_groups:\n                    unified_name = create_unified_name(group)\n                    for cat in group:\n                        level_mapping[cat] = unified_name\n\n        df[unified_col] = df[col].map(level_mapping)\n\n        num_unified = df[unified_col].notna().sum()\n        print(f\"    Created unified categories for Level {level_num} ({num_unified} mappings)\")\n\n    return df\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 5: VALIDATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef validate_pyramid_structure(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate pyramid structure\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating pyramid structure...\")\n\n    counts_by_level = {}\n\n    for level in range(1, 6):\n        col = f'unified_level_{level}'\n        # Count UNIQUE unified categories at this level, not rows\n        count = df[col].nunique()\n        counts_by_level[level] = count\n\n        if count > 0:\n            print(f\"  Level {level}: {count} unique categories\")\n\n    # Check level 1 constraint\n    if counts_by_level[1] > 0:\n        assert 10 <= counts_by_level[1] <= 20, \\\n            f\"Level 1 has {counts_by_level[1]} categories, should be 10-20\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Level 1 size valid ({counts_by_level[1]})\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_source_distribution(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate distribution of sources across taxonomy\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating source distribution...\")\n\n    source_counts = Counter(df['source'])\n    total = len(df)\n\n    print(\"  Source distribution:\")\n    for source in sorted(source_counts.keys()):\n        count = source_counts[source]\n        pct = (count / total) * 100\n        print(f\"    {source}: {count} ({pct:.1f}%)\")\n\n    # Check that no source dominates too much (max 2x ratio)\n    if len(source_counts) > 1:\n        max_count = max(source_counts.values())\n        min_count = min(source_counts.values())\n        ratio = max_count / min_count\n        assert ratio < 2.0, f\"Source imbalance too high: {ratio}x\"\n        print(f\"  ✓ Source distribution balanced (ratio: {ratio:.2f}x)\")\n\n    return True\n\n\ndef validate_naming_rules(df):\n    \"\"\"Validate naming rules\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nValidating naming rules...\")\n\n    violations = 0\n\n    # Rule: Max 5 words (separated by |)\n            words = cat.split(' | ')\n            if len(words) > 5:\n                print(f\"  ✗ {cat} has {len(words)} words (max 5)\")\n                violations += 1\n\n    if violations == 0:\n        print(f\"  ✓ All naming rules valid\")\n\n    return violations == 0\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# STEP 6: OUTPUT GENERATION\n# ============================================================================\n\ndef generate_outputs(df):\n    \"\"\"Generate output CSV files\"\"\"\n    print(\"\\nGenerating output files...\")\n\n    os.makedirs('/root/output', exist_ok=True)\n\n    #
